# AI Media Scout — 2026-10-20

Today’s edition expands stereo and spatial-video discovery and turns earlier screened entries into detailed creator guides. It covers neural editing, speech, character motion, sound design, game assets, CAD, presentations and physical pixel craft. Software licenses are separated from model, artwork, service and host restrictions. All platforms receive equal consideration; support is reported only where documented. No discovered app was installed or run, and a complete guide does not automatically become a recommendation. The library gains 46 distinct tools; 25 earlier screened entries become full guides. The complete retained catalog was scanned at the metadata level, followed by bounded manual metadata review and targeted primary-source reads beyond the automatic README shortlist, including a final pass after collection. This was not a manual eligibility review of every search result; raw unreviewed matches remain private. All 473 planned repository queries and 18 model tasks were attempted across 62 fields. Repository search outcomes: 473 successful, 0 partial and 0 failed; 110 reached result bounds. Model query failures: 0. Coverage is not exhaustive. All eight configured web ecosystems have researched findings. Some primary pages returned script shells or failed to load; these limitations are disclosed in the field and source-group notes. Model metadata is a discovery signal, not permission to publish unreviewed software. The collection records 119 warnings, including source retrieval and bounded-search disclosures retained in the report coverage details.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Field | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| Images & design | Reviewed current visual-style and restoration project pages, plus localpress and modl. Image generation, neural matting and ordinary image compression are distinguished in the new guides. [Source 1](https://curryjung.github.io/VisualStylePrompt/) [Source 2](https://herrei.github.io/localsr/) |
| Video, animation & film | Read current motion-guided video pages and editor documentation. Today’s guides cover local speech edits, AI storyboards and stereo conversion, with service-backed generation separated from local rendering. [Source 1](https://wan-move.github.io/) [Source 2](https://animationai.github.io/AnimateAnything/) |
| Audio, music & voice | Neutone’s current site documents neural audio hosting. Today’s SFX and Echo guides distinguish language-model patch authoring and optional neural stems from ordinary sound synthesis. [Source 1](https://neutone.ai/fx) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/pypi/neutone-sdk/json) |
| 3D, reconstruction & assets | Neural surface/dynamics and procedural-assembly pages remain active research context. MeshForge and Gaussian Interior Editor now have full guides with geometry, model-rights and resource limits. [Source 1](https://open3dvlab.github.io/NeuRodin/) [Source 2](https://xjay18.github.io/NeuMA_page/) [Source 3](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |
| Browser tools & web media | NodeTool describes editable agent media workflows and an alpha workspace. Some reviewed project sites expose only a JavaScript shell; a page title is not a working-demo check. [Source 1](https://nodetool.ai/) [Source 2](https://image.solusikoding.com) |
| WebXR, VR & AR | Reviewed the official IWSDK and XR Blocks AI-prototyping material, then followed stereo conversion sites. DepthVista XR has non-commercial original-code terms and is excluded. [Source 1](https://developers.meta.com/vr/blog/accelerate-vr-development-with-ai-and-immersive-web-sdk/) [Source 2](https://research.google/blog/vibe-coding-xr-accelerating-ai-xr-prototyping-with-xr-blocks-and-gemini/) |
| Computational art & creative coding | Cool-Chic documents a neural image codec and self-similarity research documents learned image representation. Neither traditional procedural effects nor AI-authored source code alone qualifies every utility as an AI tool. [Source 1](https://orange-opensource.github.io/Cool-Chic/getting_started/quickstart.html) [Source 2](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |
| Interactive, immersive & live media | Timeflux and Mind Draw Play describe biosignal-based interaction. Yvette and GNM provide concrete neural avatar paths, while model availability and input latency still need testing. [Source 1](https://timeflux.io/) [Source 2](https://www.neur0forest.art/mind-draw-play/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/VAST-AI/EdenLive) |
| 3D printing & generative CAD | The text-to-CAD site describes AI-assisted geometry exports. PindouAI adds an optional AI design route for bead patterns; mesh export and a bead grid do not establish manufacturing or color accuracy. [Source 1](https://www.texttocad.dev/) [Source 2](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |
| Games & production pipelines | Cambrian explicitly describes experimental agent-driven engine work; GardenDesigner shows scene generation. MeshForge and Scenario for Unity add asset workflows with proprietary-host/service distinctions. [Source 1](https://cambrianengine.com/) [Source 2](https://monad-cube.github.io/GardenDesigner/) |
| Motion capture & character animation | Learning2Dance and SemGeoMo describe audio/context-conditioned motion. GNM and Deadshot provide capture workflows; Deadshot’s noisy-tracking warning is a workflow limitation; its quality remains unverified without a separate explicit prototype-stage finding. [Source 1](https://verlab.github.io/Learning2Dance_CAG_2020/) [Source 2](https://4dvlab.github.io/project_page/semgeomo/) |
| Avatars, digital humans & lip sync | Read neural-motion pages and current GNM/Yvette documentation. Webcam tracking, TTS and lip animation have distinct model and hardware dependencies; a browser front end is not platform validation. [Source 1](https://4dvlab.github.io/project_page/semgeomo/) |
| VFX, compositing & relighting | Lumen and SyncLight describe neural relighting. Stereo inpainting adds another learned image-repair application, but a successful demo is not evidence of clean output on arbitrary footage. [Source 1](https://lumen-relight.github.io/) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/davidserra9/synclight) |
| Spatial audio & volumetric media | Neural acoustic fields and SoundSpaces provide spatial-audio research context. Echo’s actual AI separator is opt-in and experimental; default scene drafting is conventional signal processing. [Source 1](https://sites.google.com/view/nafs-neurips2022) [Source 2](https://vision.cs.utexas.edu/projects/soundspaces2/) [Source 3](https://openfollow.app/docs/person-detection.html) |
| Photogrammetry, scanning & neural rendering | LenslessPiCam and E2VID describe learned reconstruction from specialized sensors. Current capture guides distinguish device input, neural estimation and exported animation. [Source 1](https://lensless.readthedocs.io/) [Source 2](https://rpg.ifi.uzh.ch/E2VID.html) |
| Editing, captions & post-production | OpenFilm describes editable agent-created films. Full guides now cover dawn-cut, HyperCut, Cutbolt and DaVinci MCP; source-based setup review does not mean those apps were run. [Source 1](https://openfilm.dev/) [Source 2](https://herrei.github.io/localsr/) |
| Vector graphics, illustration & textures | VFIG and MuseDraw describe AI-assisted editable vector/diagram creation. No new independent quality evidence was established for an additional vector tool in this pass. [Source 1](https://vfig-proj.github.io/) [Source 2](https://kanjieteam.github.io/MuseDraw/) |
| Typography, fonts & layout | Strike a Chord describes diffusion-supervised glyph motion, but its project page still says code is forthcoming. AI Reels Editor’s three implemented caption styles are separated from its larger planned style list. [Source 1](https://strikeachordkt.github.io/strikeachord/) |
| Storyboarding, narrative & comics | OpenFilm’s document-based filmmaking and new VibeStudio/Hiway guides cover scene organization and model-backed creation. Hiway’s default demo is stubbed and is not a generated-film benchmark. [Source 1](https://openfilm.dev/) |
| Creative publishing & presentation | OpenPresentation documents AI-oriented presentation authoring. PPT Design & Polish adds an editable-slide workflow, and localpress adds AI alt-text and matting for WordPress media. [Source 1](https://www.openpresentation.org/docs/get-started) [Source 2](https://kanjieteam.github.io/MuseDraw/) |
| Photography, restoration & color | LocalSR and lensless-camera research provide restoration context. No unsupported claim is made that neural detail is a faithful record of the photographed subject. [Source 1](https://herrei.github.io/localsr/) [Source 2](https://lensless.readthedocs.io/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/davidserra9/synclight) |
| Data art & scientific visualization | QGIS AI Agent and VFIG supply primary map/diagram workflows. CAD and slide agents can produce editable visual communication, with factual and geometric review still required. [Source 1](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/ai_agent/) [Source 2](https://vfig-proj.github.io/) |
| Physical, robotic & kinetic installations | Ghost Arcade and biofeedback-art material were inspected for installation context. Conventional projection mapping without an identified AI stage does not qualify by itself. [Source 1](https://ghostarcade.live/) [Source 2](https://www.neur0forest.art/mind-draw-play/) [Source 3](https://openfollow.app/docs/person-detection.html) |
| Performance, projection & stage media | Neutone’s plugin host and OpenFollow’s YOLO tracking show neural performance inputs. OpenFollow explicitly documents experimental behavior and constrained tracking speed, rather than broad live-show readiness. [Source 1](https://neutone.ai/fx) [Source 2](https://openfollow.app/docs/person-detection.html) [Source 3](https://verlab.github.io/Learning2Dance_CAG_2020/) |
| Fashion, textiles & wearable media | The INTEX case describes AI-assisted material selection. It did not establish an additional license-cleared fashion-creation app during this bounded search. [Source 1](https://www.green-ai-hub.de/pilotprojekte/pilotprojekt-intex-gmbh) |
| Accessible media & assistive creation | Reviewed vocal-annotation material and a GitLab assistive-project listing; the latter is only a short script-rendered shell. Omarchy transcription, comic OCR and localpress alt text still require correction by a person. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/Jensinjames/weeablind) [Source 2](https://vak.readthedocs.io/en/latest/get_started/autoannotate.html) |
| Mobile, edge & on-device creation | SDAI’s F-Droid listing describes Android local engines and distinguishes remote generation on iOS. PindouAI documents Android releases; iOS source presence is not a validated shipped build. [Source 1](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.shifthackz.aisdv1.app.foss/) [Source 2](https://developers.meta.com/vr/blog/accelerate-vr-development-with-ai-and-immersive-web-sdk/) |
| Creative learning & authoring | Pixels2GenAI describes a media-to-generative-AI curriculum. MIT RAISE marks its site materials non-commercial; useful teaching content is not itself an open software grant. Blender Mentor’s checks remain teaching aids. [Source 1](https://pixels2genai.art/) [Source 2](https://playground.raise.mit.edu/) |
| Archives, media restoration & collections | PyPottery describes neural extraction and illustration of archaeological material. Older painting/calligraphy candidates were inspected; absent environments or missing released neural components remain gaps. [Source 1](https://lrncrd.github.io/PyPottery/) [Source 2](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/aitracer/) |
| Emerging & cross-disciplinary creative AI | The adaptive plan adds stereo/spatial video and deeper photography/stereo queries. Threadspool’s AI embroidery workflow lacks a retrieved software license; public source alone is insufficient. [Source 1](https://monad-cube.github.io/GardenDesigner/) [Source 2](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01650) |
| Tactile, vibration and haptic media | HapticGen describes text-conditioned vibration output and its training collection. No newly reviewed end-to-end actuator implementation was established; physical sensation is not verified by model examples. [Source 1](https://hapticgen.hcitech.org/) |
| Neural acoustics and responsive sound spaces | Neural acoustic fields show source/listener-dependent sound and SoundSpaces provides simulated acoustics. Echo separates learned stem extraction from deterministic spatial rendering and unproven perceptual proxies. [Source 1](https://sites.google.com/view/nafs-neurips2022) [Source 2](https://vision.cs.utexas.edu/projects/soundspaces2/) |
| AI agents for code-authored media production | OpenFilm and NodeTool describe editable agent work. New film, sound, Blender and slide guides identify the actual external model contribution and do not treat every deterministic helper as AI. [Source 1](https://openfilm.dev/) [Source 2](https://nodetool.ai/) [Source 3](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |
| AI kinetic typography and animated lettering | Strike a Chord publishes examples and a method, with code still forthcoming on the site. The implemented Reels caption styles supply a different agent-controlled typography route. [Source 1](https://strikeachordkt.github.io/strikeachord/) |
| AI choreography and dance composition | Learning2Dance and SemGeoMo document model-driven movement. Current mocap guides estimate motion rather than certify faithful choreography or injury-safe physical movement. [Source 1](https://verlab.github.io/Learning2Dance_CAG_2020/) [Source 2](https://4dvlab.github.io/project_page/semgeomo/) |
| AI-assisted textile and computational craft | COrigami and the INTEX case provide physical-design research context. PindouAI’s optional API route is concrete; threadspool has a missing license, while traditional embroidery editors are not automatically AI tools. [Source 1](https://www.green-ai-hub.de/pilotprojekte/pilotprojekt-intex-gmbh) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26299) |
| AI music notation and score recovery | BinaryMuseGAN describes learned binary piano-roll generation. No newly verified notation application was established, and waveform generation is not equivalent to editable score output. [Source 1](https://hermandong.com/bmusegan/) |
| Neural relighting and material recovery | Read Lumen and the SyncLight model card. Their examples support research relevance but do not establish that every input scene or multi-view capture can be relit accurately. [Source 1](https://lumen-relight.github.io/) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/davidserra9/synclight) |
| Olfactory and multisensory AI media | The Anemoia project and AromaGen paper describe image/multimodal-conditioned scent systems. Hardware recipes, safety, model licensing and deployment remain separate unresolved checks. [Source 1](https://www.media.mit.edu/projects/anemoia-device/overview/) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01650) |
| AI architectural visualization and spatial design | InstructScene documents instructed room layouts. Gaussian Interior Editor and AutoCAD MCP now have guides that distinguish scene exploration, reconstruction costs and concept drafting from construction-ready output. [Source 1](https://chenguolin.github.io/projects/InstructScene/) [Source 2](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) [Source 3](https://monad-cube.github.io/GardenDesigner/) |
| Neural holography and light-field media | DirectL and neural point-light-field pages describe learned display content. Rendepth documents a bounded display set; ordinary stereo output is not proof of compatibility with every light-field device. [Source 1](https://direct-l.github.io/) [Source 2](https://light.princeton.edu/publication/neural-point-light-fields/) |
| Neural rigging and digital puppetry | Iki’s site and neural-motion material provide current character context. GNM’s tracking and Yvette’s lip animation are concrete model uses; optional experimental head fitting does not label the whole tracking workflow. [Source 1](https://zeikar.dev/iki) [Source 2](https://4dvlab.github.io/project_page/semgeomo/) |
| AI ceramics and archaeological illustration | PyPottery’s primary site describes AI drawing extraction and tracing. This is documentation/heritage support, not a demonstrated ceramic-form generator or kiln-ready workflow. [Source 1](https://lrncrd.github.io/PyPottery/) |
| AI parametric design and jewelry | Procedura describes editable neural-assisted assemblies. AutoCAD MCP can expose deterministic drafting to AI agents; parameter validation does not certify fabrication constraints. [Source 1](https://www.texttocad.dev/) [Source 2](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |
| AI cartography and artistic maps | Read current QGIS AI plugin listings and AITracer descriptions. A catalog listing is discovery evidence; new eligibility still requires complete software terms and a concrete creative workflow. [Source 1](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/tags/ai/?sort=-created_on) [Source 2](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/aitracer/) |
| AI robotic painting and drawing | FRIDA describes robot-assisted painting using a learned planning approach. Hardware calibration, paint behavior and current deployment readiness remain outside documentation-only verification. [Source 1](https://pschaldenbrand.github.io/frida/) |
| AI cel animation and inbetweening | ToonComposer’s site returned only a script shell in this retrieval. No working-demo or new software-license claim is based on that shell. [Source 1](https://lg-li.github.io/project/tooncomposer/) [Source 2](https://animationai.github.io/AnimateAnything/) |
| AI Foley and sound effects | LTX-Foley’s primary docs distinguish validated LTX 2.3 from not-yet-validated 2.5. SFX’s language-model-authored synthesis patches are a different UI-sound workflow, not a foley reconstruction claim. [Source 1](https://docs.ltx.io/open-source-model/feature-guides/audio/video-to-audio-foley) |
| Learned media codecs and neural delivery | Cool-Chic’s setup documents learned image compression and the optical neural-codec research page supplies method context. Compression benchmarks are separate from creator-facing integration readiness. [Source 1](https://orange-opensource.github.io/Cool-Chic/getting_started/quickstart.html) [Source 2](https://blinorot.github.io/projects/LenslessMic/) [Source 3](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |
| AI assisted sound data art and corpus instruments | Neural acoustics and biosignal-art sources show learned mappings into sound. YOLO-sonification has concrete model names but very sparse usage documentation, so its earlier pending review is not promoted by name alone. [Source 1](https://neutone.ai/fx) |
| AI biosignal art and neurointeractive media | Timeflux and Mind Draw Play describe physiological interaction. brain2music’s EEG-to-Riffusion route remains a setup/model-rights review gap; no claim is made that it reads thoughts or reliably infers emotion. [Source 1](https://timeflux.io/) [Source 2](https://www.neur0forest.art/mind-draw-play/) |
| Neural procedural material authoring | Material Apprentice describes language-model-assisted Blender material graphs. A graph is editable, but realism, asset rights and arbitrary host compatibility need separate verification. [Source 1](https://materialapprentice.github.io/) [Source 2](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |
| Neural cameras and unconventional image capture | LenslessPiCam and E2VID require specialized acquisition data. They are learned reconstruction workflows, not evidence that any browser or ordinary camera supports them. [Source 1](https://rpg.ifi.uzh.ch/E2VID.html) [Source 2](https://lensless.readthedocs.io/) |
| AI origami, kirigami and paper engineering | COrigami describes AI-supported crease design. A paper and foldability objective do not establish licensed runnable code or a successfully fabricated fold for a user’s design. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26299) |
| AI landscape, garden and environmental design | GardenDesigner describes agent-produced garden scenes for digital environments. This does not establish civil-engineering or real planting suitability. [Source 1](https://monad-cube.github.io/GardenDesigner/) |
| Neural mosaic and stained-glass media | Neural Collages provides learned image-representation context. PindouAI distinguishes conventional quantization from optional AI motif creation and separates its restricted artwork bundle from open source code. [Source 1](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |
| AI optical illusions and anamorphic media | Visual Anagrams, Factorized Diffusion and Diffusion Illusions demonstrate model-generated perceptual transformations. Previously published unchanged tools are not recycled as discoveries. [Source 1](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/factorized_diffusion/) [Source 3](https://diffusionillusions.com/) |
| AI relief engraving and learned halftoning | Inverse-halftoning research documents learned image reconstruction. Deep Halftoning explicitly says its paper numbers are not reproducible; no readiness promotion was made. [Source 1](https://houxianxu.github.io/project/inverse-halftoning) |
| Neural brushstroke painting and drawn media | Read compositional and stylized neural-painting pages and older implementation sources. Legacy TensorFlow/Python APIs and incomplete setup instructions remain review gaps rather than undocumented install recommendations. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.03504) [Source 2](https://jiupinjia.github.io/neuralpainter/) |
| AI color direction and palette-based media | Neural Preset describes learned color mapping. DaVinci MCP’s color matching is deterministic math controlled by an AI agent, and is described accordingly. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.13511) |
| AI collage, cut-paper composition and layered media | Neural Collage Transfer and self-similarity research remain active source context. Captured-reference collages and layout utilities require a separate identified neural operation for eligibility. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.02202) [Source 2](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |
| AI stereo conversion and spatial video authoring | Read Rendepth, StereoPilot and M2SVid primary sites, then their source/setup terms. New guides distinguish depth warping, learned novel-view synthesis, light-field playback and model-specific rights. Two restrictive-code apps and two missing-license repos remain excluded or held. [Source 1](https://rendepth.com/) [Source 2](https://hit-perfect.github.io/StereoPilot/) [Source 3](https://m2svid.github.io/) |

## Search and review counts

62 creative fields; 473/473 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 25708 distinct source candidates and 847 model leads. 45 detailed profiles and 26 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 110 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Read the first fifty neural and diffusion project API results. Several are mirrors; a Foundation-1 audio-node candidate needs further source/license review, and its assumed GitHub counterpart was unavailable. This bounded listing is not an exhaustive crawl. [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects?search=neural&simple=true&per_page=50) [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects?search=diffusion&simple=true&per_page=50)
- **codeberg** · searched: Read fifty neural repository results through the primary API. Colorization and ASR bindings were among candidates; no additional complete creative-app license/setup review was completed. [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=neural&limit=50)
- **sourcehut** · searched: Read the audio project listing. Most visible matches were conventional audio/DSP players or infrastructure; no additional clearly AI creator application was cleared. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=audio)
- **packages** · searched: Read Neutone SDK and DeepFilterNet JSON metadata, plus the SDAI F-Droid entry. Neutone metadata contains conflicting license labels and DeepFilterNet supplied no description in this response; package metadata alone does not clear code or model terms. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/neutone-sdk/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/deepfilternet/json) [Source](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.shifthackz.aisdv1.app.foss/)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Reviewed QGIS listings, Neutone, ComfyStereo, LongVideo SBS, the Unity integration and agent editing plugins. Host/application, source, model and runtime permissions remain distinct. [Source](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/aitracer/) [Source](https://neutone.ai/fx) [Source](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo) [Source](https://github.com/edemaistre/unity-plugin)
- **project-sites** · searched: Retrieved primary pages across all sixty-two observed fields and followed stereo, craft and editor sources. ToonComposer and Racik exposed only short script shells; modl documentation returned 403 while its main site and repository remained readable. Deadshot’s linked report returned 404. These are explicit availability gaps. [Source](https://rendepth.com/) [Source](https://hit-perfect.github.io/StereoPilot/) [Source](https://m2svid.github.io/) [Source](https://modl.run) [Source](https://localpress.griffen.codes)
- **research-code** · searched: Reviewed stereo synthesis, neural painting, biosignal media and physical-craft research. Research origin does not mean Experimental; an explicit unstable/current-prototype statement is required. Missing implementation or source-license evidence remains a hold. [Source](https://m2svid.github.io/) [Source](https://hit-perfect.github.io/StereoPilot/) [Source](https://github.com/Nikronic/Deep-Halftoning) [Source](https://github.com/alexandra03/threadspool)
- **international** · searched: Read Korean comic-translation and production docs, Vietnamese TTS, Chinese bead-design and mocap docs, and a German textile case. Language and star count are not quality verdicts. [Source](https://github.com/UCX0204/CarrotMangaTranslator) [Source](https://github.com/BsMediaVN/studio-agent) [Source](https://github.com/xuange6610/PindouAI) [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap)

## dawn-cut

Editing, captions & post-production · Video, animation & film · Accessible media & assistive creation

First complete guide, expanding an earlier screened library entry. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-20; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Cut narrated footage by editing its transcript; make Korean captions and short reframed versions.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README links a reproducible demo-output folder; the hero GIF remains a placeholder. [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/demo-output/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install Node 20+, pnpm 10, CMake and FFmpeg, then install the workspace and prepare whisper.cpp plus a model. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/demo-output/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT software and whisper.cpp; FFmpeg build terms are separate. Optional Qwen2.5-1.5B planner is documented as Apache-2.0; sample photos have separate terms. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/demo-output/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. This guide covers transcript editing. The optional LLM planner and MCP server are explicitly experimental; MCP rendering lacks subtitle/overlay burn-in, and multitrack is unfinished.

### How it uses AI

Local Whisper transcription drives word-level video cuts, subtitle correction and caption export. [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/demo-output/README.md)

### Install

Install Node 20+, pnpm 10, CMake and FFmpeg, then install the workspace and prepare whisper.cpp plus a model. [Source 1](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/demo-output/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Node 20+, pnpm 10, CMake and FFmpeg, then install the workspace and prepare whisper.cpp plus a model.

```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm setup:binaries
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/kwakseongjae/dawn-cut/blob/main/demo-output/README.md)

1. Import a short recording and transcribe it.
2. Correct low-confidence words, delete unwanted text and preview the resulting timeline.
3. Export a captioned video plus SRT and save the .dawn project.

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. - **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node 20+, pnpm 10, CMake, FFmpeg and whisper.cpp. Default large-v3-turbo download is about 1.6 GB; base is a documented lighter option.
- **Platforms:** Electron desktop source; the fixture and default speech-output route use macOS say. Equivalent Windows/Linux end-to-end behavior is not established here.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The project site identifies the application as alpha. Pod workflows currently omit ControlNet/style-reference and inpainting masks; some vision models load separately downloaded code.

### How it uses AI

A managed local runtime combines diffusion image generation, editing, model storage and LoRA training behind a CLI and browser UI. [Source](https://github.com/modl-org/modl/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://modl.run) [Source](https://modl.run/docs)

### Introduction

A managed local runtime combines diffusion image generation, editing, model storage and LoRA training behind a CLI and browser UI. [Source 1](https://github.com/modl-org/modl/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://modl.run) [Source 3](https://modl.run/docs)

### What it is good for

Develop an image style or product LoRA, then generate variations through a repeatable local workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/modl-org/modl/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://modl.run) [Source 3](https://modl.run/docs)

### How it uses AI

Neural depth estimated from cubemap faces drives stereo reconstruction of monoscopic 360-degree footage. ### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/examples/Video2Stereo.json)

- **Hardware:** StereoDiffusion asks for a CUDA GPU with at least 8 GB VRAM, recommends 16 GB, and downloads roughly 4 GB of model data. System RAM is unspecified.
- **Software:** Working ComfyUI and node requirements. StereoDiffusion documents Python 3.8+ and PyTorch 2.0+; OpenXR viewing needs an active compatible runtime.
- **Platforms:** CUDA generation is documented, with CPU fallback for some depth processing. The headset list is an author support claim, not a tested OS/headset matrix.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 4](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/examples/Video2Stereo.json)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT original code plus Apache-2.0 prompt-to-prompt components; retain NOTICE. Stable Diffusion and depth-model weights have separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT original code plus Apache-2.0 prompt-to-prompt components; retain NOTICE. Stable Diffusion and depth-model weights have separate terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/Dobidop/ComfyStereo/blob/main/examples/Video2Stereo.json)

### Limitations

StereoDiffusion currently supports SD1.x/SD2.x; SDXL/FLUX support is planned. Headset and output-quality claims were not tested. - **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Optional Whisper weights and FFmpeg distributions need their own terms. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Author-reported Resolve Studio 21 and import tests are not independent testing. Live scripting is unavailable in free Resolve. No guarantee that a metric or automated grade matches an editor’s judgment.

### How it uses AI

An Apache-licensed MCP server lets an AI agent assemble edits, search spoken footage and prepare captions, LUTs and audio mixes for DaVinci Resolve. Its numeric color, timing and export engines are deterministic; the AI role is the connected agent and optional local transcription. [Source](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE)

### Introduction

An Apache-licensed MCP server lets an AI agent assemble edits, search spoken footage and prepare captions, LUTs and audio mixes for DaVinci Resolve. Its numeric color, timing and export engines are deterministic; the AI role is the connected agent and optional local transcription. [Source 1](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE)

### What it is good for

Agent-assisted music-video rough cuts, interview selects, reference-color workflows and delivery preparation. [Source 1](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE)

### Demo & examples

The official README contains a before/reference/matched color strip, a seven-call agent session and a waveform example. These are author demonstrations, not independent reliability tests. [Source 1](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE)

### Install

Install the Python package from the official repository and provide FFmpeg. Register unofficial-davinci-mcp with an MCP client. Live editing requires a running licensed Resolve Studio with local external scripting enabled; the free edition instead imports FCPXML, EDL, markers and LUTs. [Source 1](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the Python package from the official repository and provide FFmpeg. Register unofficial-davinci-mcp with an MCP client. Live editing requires a running licensed Resolve Studio with local external scripting enabled; the free edition instead imports FCPXML, EDL, markers and LUTs.

```sh
pip install "git+https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp.git"
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE)

1. Call resolve_capabilities and confirm the live or interchange tier.
2. Probe a short set of clips, ask for an edit and inspect the default dry-run plan.
3. Apply a reviewed plan or import the generated interchange files; check captions, cuts, color and loudness in Resolve.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/wassermanproductions/unofficial-davinci-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+, NumPy, FFmpeg; optional librosa for beat tracking and faster-whisper for the voice bridge; an MCP-compatible AI client.
- **Platforms:** macOS and Linux are documented. The push-to-talk bridge is specifically macOS. Windows support is not established here.

An object-level Gaussian-splat scene editor combines reconstructed rooms, segmentation, CLIP recognition and an optional local language-model agent for interior exploration. ### How it uses AI

A neural stereo-conversion pipeline predicts depth, warps a right-eye view and uses a modified Stable Video Diffusion network for learned inpainting and refinement. [Source](https://github.com/google-research/m2svid/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://m2svid.github.io/)

### Introduction

A neural stereo-conversion pipeline predicts depth, warps a right-eye view and uses a modified Stable Video Diffusion network for learned inpainting and refinement. [Source 1](https://github.com/google-research/m2svid/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://m2svid.github.io/)

### What it is good for

Research-oriented monocular-to-stereo video conversion and disocclusion repair. [Source 1](https://github.com/google-research/m2svid/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://m2svid.github.io/)

### Demo & examples

The project page shows input, generated right-eye, anaglyph and downloadable stereo examples, plus an author-run user study. Those comparisons do not establish independent use. [Source 1](https://github.com/google-research/m2svid/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://m2svid.github.io/)

Follow the two-environment recipe: DepthCrafter plus the sgm environment.yml. Obtain the Hi3D/OpenCLIP prerequisites and released M2SVid weights before running the provided inference script. [Source 1](https://github.com/google-research/m2svid/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://m2svid.github.io/)

### How it uses AI

Echo can optionally call Demucs to separate a mix into stems, which a developer can explicitly place in its sound-scene graph for spatial rendering. [Source 1](https://github.com/SirLichDevCraft/PixelHand/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SirLichDevCraft/PixelHand/blob/main/docs/guide.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the repository with Git and use Node.js 20.3+. The authored lantern example needs neither dependency installation nor a model key. For AI editing, connect node src/mcp.js as a stdio MCP server with the core toolset, or configure the documented Ollama, LM Studio or compatible API adapter.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/SirLichDevCraft/PixelHand.git
```


```sh
cd PixelHand
```


```sh
node examples/lantern.js
```


```sh
node src/mcp.js
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/SirLichDevCraft/PixelHand/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/SirLichDevCraft/PixelHand/blob/main/docs/guide.md)

1. Open the authored lantern output to understand the editable project format.
2. Connect an AI client, initialize a project and ask for a small sprite within a fixed palette.
3. Inspect silhouettes and native-size renders after each edit; review every facing and frame before exporting.

## 3DTopia-XL

3D, reconstruction & assets · Neural procedural material authoring · Games & production pipelines

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-20; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Exploring image-conditioned 3D props and textured GLB assets; a separate text-conditioned path is documented for developers.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/LICENSE.txt)
- **Creative results · documented:** The project page and model card show object turntables and a hosted Gradio demo. The reported five-second denoising stage is not an end-to-end runtime guarantee. [Source](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://3dtopia.github.io/3DTopia-XL/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/FrozenBurning/3DTopia-XL)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Create the documented Python 3.9 Conda environment, activate it, install PyTorch 2.1.2 with CUDA 11.8, xformers and the requirements, then build the third-party libraries. The Gradio entry point downloads its image-conditioned weights; CLI use needs the VAE and DiT files under pretrained. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://3dtopia.github.io/3DTopia-XL/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/FrozenBurning/3DTopia-XL)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Code and the reviewed image-model card identify Apache-2.0. The text conditioner, third-party rendering libraries and individual Objaverse assets have separate terms; the training collection is not a blanket artwork grant. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://3dtopia.github.io/3DTopia-XL/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/FrozenBurning/3DTopia-XL)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The text route requires code/configuration adaptation, not simply changing a UI toggle. Older Trimesh versions need the documented material-packing fix. A textured export is not proof of watertight or print-ready geometry.

### How it uses AI

A diffusion-transformer system generates geometry and physically based material assets using a learned primitive representation. [Source](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://3dtopia.github.io/3DTopia-XL/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/FrozenBurning/3DTopia-XL)

### Introduction

A diffusion-transformer system generates geometry and physically based material assets using a learned primitive representation. [Source 1](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dtopia.github.io/3DTopia-XL/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/FrozenBurning/3DTopia-XL)

### What it is good for

Exploring image-conditioned 3D props and textured GLB assets; a separate text-conditioned path is documented for developers. [Source 1](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dtopia.github.io/3DTopia-XL/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/FrozenBurning/3DTopia-XL)

### Demo & examples

The project page and model card show object turntables and a hosted Gradio demo. The reported five-second denoising stage is not an end-to-end runtime guarantee. [Source 1](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dtopia.github.io/3DTopia-XL/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/FrozenBurning/3DTopia-XL)

### Install

Create the documented Python 3.9 Conda environment, activate it, install PyTorch 2.1.2 with CUDA 11.8, xformers and the requirements, then build the third-party libraries. The Gradio entry point downloads its image-conditioned weights; CLI use needs the VAE and DiT files under pretrained. [Source 1](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dtopia.github.io/3DTopia-XL/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/FrozenBurning/3DTopia-XL)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Create the documented Python 3.9 Conda environment, activate it, install PyTorch 2.1.2 with CUDA 11.8, xformers and the requirements, then build the third-party libraries. The Gradio entry point downloads its image-conditioned weights; CLI use needs the VAE and DiT files under pretrained.

```sh
conda create -n primx python=3.9
```


```sh
conda activate primx
```


```sh
conda install pytorch==2.1.2 torchvision==0.16.2 torchaudio==2.1.2 pytorch-cuda=11.8 -c pytorch -c nvidia
```


```sh
conda install xformers::xformers
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
bash install.sh
```


```sh
python app.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/3DTopia/3DTopia-XL/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dtopia.github.io/3DTopia-XL/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/FrozenBurning/3DTopia-XL)

1. Prepare a clear image of one object and use the Gradio interface or edit input_dir in configs/inference_dit.yml.
2. Adjust sampling steps and guidance, then enable export_glb.
3. Inspect geometry, UV/material packing and silhouettes in a 3D editor; retopologize where necessary.

[Source](https://github.com/showlab/MotionDirector/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://showlab.github.io/MotionDirector/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/cerspense/zeroscope_v2_576w) [Source](https://huggingface.co/ruizhaocv/MotionDirector)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Older Python/dependencies and mismatched checkpoint generations can hinder reproduction. Published examples are short clips; image fidelity and temporal consistency are not assured.

### How it uses AI

Learns motion from reference clips by adapting spatial and temporal paths in a text-to-video diffusion model. ### Introduction

```sh
conda create -n motiondirector python=3.8
```

Combines 2D and 3D diffusion priors to optimize a textured 3D object from a single image. [Source 1](https://github.com/guochengqian/Magic123/blob/main/readme.md) [Source 2](https://guochengqian.github.io/project/magic123/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/cvlab/zero123-weights) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/stable-diffusion-v1-5/stable-diffusion-v1-5)

1. Start with an included preprocessed example; custom images need foreground/depth preprocessing.
2. Use the documented coarse/fine configuration, optionally skipping textual inversion with its stated fidelity tradeoff.
3. Export with --save_mesh and inspect the OBJ, material and texture files in a 3D viewer.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/guochengqian/Magic123/blob/main/readme.md) [Source 2](https://guochengqian.github.io/project/magic123/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/cvlab/zero123-weights) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/stable-diffusion-v1-5/stable-diffusion-v1-5)

- **Hardware:** The documented full example uses a 32 GB V100 and reports roughly 40 minutes coarse plus 20 minutes fine, with additional hours for optional textual inversion. This is not a stated minimum; system RAM and disk totals are unspecified.
- **Software:** NVIDIA CUDA, PyTorch, matching compiler and Python headers, Git, wget and Eigen. The current guide discusses Blackwell/CUDA 12.8+ and compiler compatibility; it also contains older model paths.
- **Platforms:** Ubuntu is explicitly tested. Windows compiler notes and Colab guidance exist but do not establish a fully tested native Windows workflow. macOS support is not documented.

- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 code. Zero123 is MIT-labeled but its card also states research-purpose intent and excluded uses; Stable Diffusion 1.5 uses OpenRAIL terms. MiDaS and other dependencies are separate. No blanket commercial clearance is inferred.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 code. Zero123 is MIT-labeled but its card also states research-purpose intent and excluded uses; Stable Diffusion 1.5 uses OpenRAIL terms. MiDaS and other dependencies are separate. No blanket commercial clearance is inferred.

### How it uses AI

Trains a DINO-based keypoint/surface detector on synthetic views of a rig, then fits its bones to two-view video and exports animation. - **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT code; DINOv2 is separately Apache-2.0. Optional DINOv3 is gated under its own terms. The example cat/video came from Tripo and Seedance; attribution is supplied but a broad asset redistribution grant is not established. Use your own cleared inputs.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT code; DINOv2 is separately Apache-2.0. Optional DINOv3 is gated under its own terms. The example cat/video came from Tripo and Seedance; attribution is supplied but a broad asset redistribution grant is not established. Use your own cleared inputs.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/sparklecatta-lang/quadmocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/sparklecatta-lang/quadmocap/blob/main/examples/cat/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/facebook/dinov2-base)

### Limitations

Generic-rig support has only limited example testing. Raw actions jitter, feet can slide and fast/deformed views degrade results; an optional postprocessing recipe is not a guarantee. Two-view video generation is an external stage. [Source 1](https://github.com/sparklecatta-lang/quadmocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/sparklecatta-lang/quadmocap/blob/main/examples/cat/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/facebook/dinov2-base)

The author reports testing on their Windows 11, RTX 3090 and UE 5.8 machine; this is not broad compatibility evidence. [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/ue5-ai-mocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md)

Install GVHMR inside WSL with its specified checkpoints, register for the body models, then preview setup, write local paths and run the dependency doctor. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/ue5-ai-mocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md)

- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT integration source. Required GVHMR and SMPL/SMPL-X components are non-commercial research dependencies; commercial use needs separate authorization. Unreal/MetaHuman have separate host terms, and optional Dreamina/Seedance is a paid service. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/ue5-ai-mocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. This is an open-source integration around a restricted research stack, not an unrestricted commercial mocap solution. Finger shapes are coarse states, fast crossings can deform and manual correction is needed. The plugin UI is Chinese.

### How it uses AI

An integration pipeline estimates two-person motion with GVHMR, cleans it and retargets animation into Unreal MetaHuman sequences. [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/ue5-ai-mocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md)

### Introduction

An integration pipeline estimates two-person motion with GVHMR, cleans it and retargets animation into Unreal MetaHuman sequences. [Source 1](https://github.com/darcyjia/ue5-ai-mocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md)

### What it is good for

Researching two-character action previz from an existing video, with optional paid generated-video input. [Source 1](https://github.com/darcyjia/ue5-ai-mocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md)

### Demo & examples

The repository links a thirteen-minute installation/output walkthrough and describes quality-report outputs. The author reports testing on their Windows 11, RTX 3090 and UE 5.8 machine; this is not broad compatibility evidence. [Source 1](https://github.com/darcyjia/ue5-ai-mocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md)

### Install

Follow the fresh INSTALL guide: prepare UE 5.8, Python 3.13, a separate Python 3.12 hand-tracking environment, Blender, FFmpeg and WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04. Install GVHMR inside WSL with its specified checkpoints, register for the body models, then preview setup, write local paths and run the dependency doctor. [Source 1](https://github.com/darcyjia/ue5-ai-mocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Follow the fresh INSTALL guide: prepare UE 5.8, Python 3.13, a separate Python 3.12 hand-tracking environment, Blender, FFmpeg and WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04. Install GVHMR inside WSL with its specified checkpoints, register for the body models, then preview setup, write local paths and run the dependency doctor.

```sh
python -m aimocap setup --project D:/MyProj/MyProj.uproject
```


```sh
python -m aimocap setup --project D:/MyProj/MyProj.uproject --write --install-plugin
```


```sh
python -m aimocap setup --install-tools
```


```sh
python -m aimocap doctor
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/darcyjia/ue5-ai-mocap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/darcyjia/UE5-AI-Mocap/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md)

1. Use an existing cleared video to avoid the optional generation service, and create a named motion job.
2. Select the two characters, solve motion and review tracking, foot placement and retargeting metrics.
3. Inspect the dedicated Unreal sequence and correct hands/impacts before a preview render; close the editor before the command-line UE asset-writing stage.

- **Hardware:** The documented test machine uses an RTX 3090 with 24 GB VRAM. This is not a published minimum; total RAM, disk and supported alternative GPUs are unspecified.
- **Software:** UE 5.8 with MetaHuman, Windows Python 3.13 and 3.12, Blender 4.x, FFmpeg, WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 and the separate GVHMR environment.
- **Platforms:** Only Windows 10/11 plus NVIDIA is described as tested in the installation guide. Standalone Linux and macOS support are not established.

Creating glyph-based motion art through a model-directed preview/edit/render loop, alongside manual browser controls. [Source 1](https://github.com/xand0dev/ascii-render/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://xand0dev.github.io/ascii-render/?demo)

### Demo & examples

The repository links a browser demo, procedural example reel and preset gallery. These demonstrate the renderer rather than autonomous model judgment. [Source 1](https://github.com/xand0dev/ascii-render/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://xand0dev.github.io/ascii-render/?demo)

### Install

For the AI route, use Node 20+ and FFmpeg, install dependencies and build the MCP server. Point a stdio MCP client at the generated server.mjs using absolute paths where needed. The browser editor has its own npm development/build commands. [Source 1](https://github.com/xand0dev/ascii-render/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://xand0dev.github.io/ascii-render/?demo)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For the AI route, use Node 20+ and FFmpeg, install dependencies and build the MCP server. Point a stdio MCP client at the generated server.mjs using absolute paths where needed. The browser editor has its own npm development/build commands.

```sh
npm install
```


```sh
npm run build:mcp
```


```sh
node /absolute/path/to/ascii-render/mcp/dist/server.mjs
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/xand0dev/ascii-render/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://xand0dev.github.io/ascii-render/?demo)

1. Ask the connected assistant to inspect video_info and preview_frame for a short local clip.
2. Compare a few styles and adjust glyph density, color and motion effects using preview images.
3. Render a short MP4/GIF and inspect audio, crop and timing before committing to a longer export.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/xand0dev/ascii-render/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://xand0dev.github.io/ascii-render/?demo)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node.js 20+, FFmpeg/FFprobe and an MCP client/model; browser export relies on WebCodecs while the Node server uses a canvas runtime and FFmpeg.
- **Platforms:** The browser and Node routes are distinct. macOS path examples are documented, but a complete native-host/browser codec support matrix is not supplied.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/xand0dev/ascii-render/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/xand0dev/ascii-render/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://xand0dev.github.io/ascii-render/?demo)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT application code. Font, FFmpeg build and external AI-client/model terms remain separate. Processing/rendering is local; that does not establish that an external AI client keeps preview images on-device.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT application code. Font, FFmpeg build and external AI-client/model terms remain separate. Processing/rendering is local; that does not establish that an external AI client keeps preview images on-device.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/xand0dev/ascii-render/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://xand0dev.github.io/ascii-render/?demo)

### Limitations

The renderer itself is conventional image/signal processing; inclusion is for the explicit visual AI control loop. Real-time 4K WebGL preview and folder batch processing are roadmap items. No hardware minimum or complete export-compatibility test was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/xand0dev/ascii-render/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://xand0dev.github.io/ascii-render/?demo)

## Content Machine

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · AI kinetic typography and animated lettering

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-20; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Making reviewable captioned shorts and motion graphics from real footage and transcripts with an agent’s editorial input.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The native-renderer GIF is explicitly a synthetic component demonstration, not customer footage or a one-click editing benchmark. Example plans provide a reproduction starting point. [Source](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Prepare Python 3.12, Node 22 or 24, FFmpeg/FFprobe with libx264/libass and an installed Chrome-family browser. Windows uses setup.ps1; Linux/macOS use the documented virtual environment, locked Python requirements and npm ci in the graphics directory. Run the project doctor before editing. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT project code. Remotion is separately licensed and may require payment depending on use; the MIT grant does not cover it. FFmpeg, fonts, models, browser and source footage retain separate terms. FFmpeg-only steps can avoid the optional graphics framework where supported. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. JSON validation and technical QA do not prove good editorial choices or factual narration. The demonstration is synthetic, and broader platform/output quality remains unverified.

### How it uses AI

A local editing engine lets an AI coding agent write explicit JSON editorial plans and renders them with FFmpeg and optional Remotion graphics. [Source](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Introduction

A local editing engine lets an AI coding agent write explicit JSON editorial plans and renders them with FFmpeg and optional Remotion graphics. [Source 1](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### What it is good for

Making reviewable captioned shorts and motion graphics from real footage and transcripts with an agent’s editorial input. [Source 1](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Demo & examples

The native-renderer GIF is explicitly a synthetic component demonstration, not customer footage or a one-click editing benchmark. Example plans provide a reproduction starting point. [Source 1](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Install

Prepare Python 3.12, Node 22 or 24, FFmpeg/FFprobe with libx264/libass and an installed Chrome-family browser. Windows uses setup.ps1; Linux/macOS use the documented virtual environment, locked Python requirements and npm ci in the graphics directory. Run the project doctor before editing. [Source 1](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Prepare Python 3.12, Node 22 or 24, FFmpeg/FFprobe with libx264/libass and an installed Chrome-family browser. Windows uses setup.ps1; Linux/macOS use the documented virtual environment, locked Python requirements and npm ci in the graphics directory. Run the project doctor before editing.

```sh
python3.12 -m venv .venv
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python -m pip install -r requirements.lock.txt
```


```sh
npm --prefix motore/video-gfx ci
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python tools/content_machine.py doctor
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Provide a short local video, transcript and explicit editing brief to the connected agent.
2. Review its JSON cut/caption/overlay plan and generated contact sheets before rendering.
3. Inspect the exported clip and technical QA, then revise crops, retained words or timing through the plan.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/giuseppedellipaoli/content-machine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.12, Node 22/24, FFmpeg/FFprobe with the documented codecs, Chrome/Chromium/Edge. An external coding agent supplies the AI; optional faster-whisper supplies local transcription.
- **Platforms:** The author verified a clean installation/render on Windows. Linux and macOS setup instructions exist but are explicitly unverified end to end.

### Manga Cleaner · GPL-3.0

Preparing authorized comic pages for localization or lettering revisions.
Uses neural text/bubble detection and manga-LaMa inpainting to remove selected lettering from manga and webtoon pages, preserving editable regional layers.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. GPL-3.0-only application; separate model downloads retain their own terms. Apple Silicon is the measured route; Windows/Linux runtime and GPU claims remain unmeasured, Intel Mac is unsupported. The README documents about 317 MB of required weights, plus runtime and optional models; these are download sizes, not RAM minima. Flat-fill is conventional processing, optional cloud/FLUX routes have additional dependencies, and full model/setup review is pending.

### Convertino AI Cut-out · MIT

Extracting subjects and text while preparing image assets from a desktop file workflow.
Its optional BiRefNet-lite model performs local neural background removal; its OCR path uses platform recognition engines or Tesseract.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT original code with an inspected dependency notice. Most file conversion and editing actions are conventional; inclusion is limited to the neural cut-out/OCR routes. The optional cut-out model is a 224 MB download with separate upstream terms; GPU acceleration is documented on Windows, CPU on Linux and Apple Silicon, and the Intel Mac build lacks cut-out. macOS packages are unsigned/not notarized and the installer changes quarantine flags. Full setup, model terms and output-quality review remain pending.

## Excluded and unresolved findings

Research notes only; these do not enter the eligible tool library.

- **DepthVista XR** · excluded: The complete license places original additions under CC BY-NC 4.0. Permissive dependencies do not make those additions open source. [Source](https://github.com/Bastian-Noel/DepthVistaXR/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **VisionDepth3D** · excluded: The complete EULA restricts redistribution, modification and uses of its free tier; this is not an open-source software grant. [Source](https://github.com/VisionDepth/VisionDepth3D/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **3D-Converter and stereo-3d** · needs-license-review: Metadata/readme were available but complete software-license retrieval returned 404 for both. No library inclusion is granted. [Source](https://github.com/jdmarinv/3D-Converter) [Source](https://github.com/ChrisChowen/stereo-3d)
- **Threadspool** · needs-license-review: An LLM embroidery-agent workflow is concrete, but a complete software license was not retrieved. [Source](https://github.com/alexandra03/threadspool)
- **dramai license wording** · needs-license-review: The nominal Apache-2.0 file has substantive wording differences from the standard text, including the trademark exception and liability section. Its prior screened record is preserved; no new full guide or license clearance is granted pending clarification. [Source](https://github.com/hyyyyyyz/dramai/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **OpenStitch and Lilo craft pages** · excluded: OpenStitch describes AI autodigitizing as in progress; Lilo’s inspected workflow is conventional embroidery tracing. A promised AI feature or ordinary generative craft does not establish a currently available AI tool. [Source](https://dmplus.io/openstitch/) [Source](https://lilo.kadwood.com/)
- **GANCalligraphy** · needs-license-review: The app lists generation/style-transfer features, but their neural implementations are still marked as not released. Its Android shell does not establish a reproducible fully open creative-AI stack. [Source](https://github.com/baoqianyue/GANCalligraphy)
- **Older neural art and biosignal setup gaps** · needs-license-review: Reviewed Deep Halftoning, coordinate-network painting, pix2pix-watercolor, Font Generation, brain2music and YOLO sonification. Incomplete or legacy environments, sparse instructions and unresolved model/data rights prevent a readiness promotion; the earlier review queue is retained. [Source](https://github.com/Nikronic/Deep-Halftoning) [Source](https://github.com/guiferviz/nn_painting_tensorflow) [Source](https://github.com/tallivm/brain2music)
- **Open Design license wording** · needs-license-review: The complete nominal Apache-2.0 file removes wording from the trademark exception and replaces liability wording. This modified text was not cleared as a standard open-source grant. [Source](https://github.com/nexu-io/open-design/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **FACEGOOD Audio2Face distribution** · needs-license-review: The repository code has an MIT license, but its README places the test software and Unreal project under non-commercial terms. The integrated demo distribution is not cleared as an open-source creative tool. [Source](https://github.com/FACEGOOD/FACEGOOD-Audio2Face)
- **Stagelight usage wording** · needs-license-review: The root code license is MIT, but the README legal section limits the tool to personal/private use and says not to run it as a public service. Music/video rights are also separate. This apparent scope conflict needs clarification before adding it as a cleared open-source application. [Source](https://github.com/bartoszkolo/stagelight/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/bartoszkolo/stagelight)
- **myUpscaler media-engine permission** · needs-license-review: The app code is Apache-2.0, but its required MpVFX media engine is proprietary. The engine file says download alone grants no use, copying or redistribution permission; the README says the app includes it by permission. That end-user permission scope needs clarification before this workflow is cleared. [Source](https://github.com/marshiyar/myUpscaler) [Source](https://github.com/marshiyar/myUpscaler/blob/main/myUpscaler/ThirdParty/MpVFX-Media-LICENSE.txt)
- **Voxr AI handoff scope** · excluded: The inspected core performs deterministic subtitle/audio measurements and exports generic regeneration prompts. It explicitly does not call the named TTS tools. Its included agent-review skill needs a separate substantive review before an AI workflow is cleared; being useful after AI narration is not sufficient alone. [Source](https://github.com/dlxeva/voxr)
